The matrix indexing is column-major: idx2(i,j,n) = j*n+i means element at row i, col j stored at j*n+i. Loop order k outer, i middle, j inner. Inner loop over j accesses dist[idx2(j,i)] = dist[i*n + j]? Wait idx2(j,i,n) = i*n + j. So inner j accesses consecutive memory at offset i*n + j — contiguous. distIK = dist[idx2(k,i)] = i*n + k (fixed for inner loop). distKJ = dist[idx2(j,k)] = k*n + j (contiguous inner). So good memory access. path update idx2(j,i) contiguous.\n\nParallelize over i (outer middle loop) with OpenMP. Each i iteration writes distinct row i of dist and path. For a fixed k, dist[i][j] update depends on dist[i][k] and dist[k][j]. The value dist[i][k] is constant over j, but it can itself be updated during this k iteration (when j=k, dist[i][k] might be improved). In Floyd-Warshall, standard in-place is correct because dist[i][k] and dist[k][j] cannot be improved via k. dist[i][k] via k: dist[i][k] + dist[k][k] where dist[k][k]=0, so no improvement. So safe. Parallelizing over i independent since each i writes its own row; reads dist[k][j] (row k, fixed) and dist[i][k] from own row. Within k iteration, row i's element k may change while other threads read? Each thread reads only its own row i (distIK) and row k. Row k is written by thread handling i=k. But row k writes could race with other threads reading dist[k][j] for j... However row k's values cannot change during iteration k (since dist[k][j] + dist[k][k] = dist[k][j], no improvement, because dist[k][k]=0). But is dist[k][k] guaranteed 0? Initially 0, and never changed (newDist = distIK+distKJ >= 0, never < 0). So row k never changes. Also with path, path[k][j] could change? The if newDist < distIJ is false for row k, so no. Good.\n\nSo parallelize over i, collapse? Better cache tiling perhaps. Benchmark likely runs n=512 or larger. Standard approach: parallelize i loop. For maximum performance, use cache blocking over i and j, plus OpenMP on i. But simpler: `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` on i with default simd inner. There are two arrays dist and path both written. Could split into distance-only inner vectorized and path update. But semantics require path updated only when dist improved. We can compute condition vectorized for dist, but path needs same condition. Compiler might vectorize with masked stores. Possibly use `#pragma omp simd` and hope. Simpler: process full blocks of size B and then? Remainder handling complicates. Could choose B that divides n? n arbitrary. General blocked FW handles partial blocks with bounds min. E.g., block start/end arrays. Implementation with variable block sizes is doable.\n\nFor each K block:\n  kStart=K*B, kEnd=min(n,kStart+B)\n  #pragma omp single\n  for k in [kStart,kEnd):\n    // diagonal block update: for i,j in [kStart,kEnd)\n    ...\n  barrier implicit after single\n\n  // Phase 2: update column block K rows (all i not in K? Actually update dist[i][j] for j in K-block, all i outside K, using k in block)\n  #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n  for (I=0; I<nb; ++I) if I != K:\n     iStart..iEnd\n     for k in kStart..kEnd:\n        for i in iStart..iEnd:\n           distIK = dist[i*n+k]\n           for j in kStart..kEnd:\n              new = distIK + dist[k*n+j]; if < dist[i*n+j] update\n  barrier\n\n  // Phase 3: update row block K (all j outside K): for each J != K, for k in block, for i in block, for j in J:\n  #pragma omp for\n  for J != K:\n     for k:\n        for i in kStart..kEnd:\n           distIK=dist[i*n+k]\n           for j in jStart..jEnd:\n  barrier\n\n  // Phase 4: off-diagonal blocks\n  #pragma omp for collapse(2) schedule(dynamic?) \n  for I in 0..nb, I!=K:\n    for J in 0..nb, J!=K:\n       for k in block:\n         for i in I:\n            distIK...\n            for j in J:\n  barrier\n\nNote the row/column phases: To exactly match plain FW within block, phase 2 updates column block K using k in order; phase 3 row block K; phase 4 after both. Plain FW order: for k in block: update all i,j. Blocked does for phase2 all k for column block, then phase3 all k for row block, then phase4 all k.
